<!-- page 1 -->
NOTICE OF PUBLIC HEARING AND AGENDA
NAVAJO COUNTY BOARD OF SUPERVISORS
Tuesday, December 12, 2017
ONE OR MORE SUPERVISORS MAY ATTEND VIA VIDEO AND/OR TELEPHONICALLY 
The Navajo County Board of Supervisors will hold a meeting open to the public on Tuesday, December 12, 2017, in the Supervisors’
Chamber in the Navajo County Governmental Complex, Holbrook, Arizona. The Board may vote to meet in a closed executive
session to discuss certain matters as indicated on the following agenda. The Board may meet in executive session pursuant to
A.R.S. § 38-431.03(A) (3) for legal advice on any item.
Persons with disabilities who need accommodation to attend or participate in the meeting may contact the Clerk of the Board’s office
at (928) 524-4053; at least 48 hours prior to the meeting so an accommodation can be arranged.
Items on the agenda may be considered out of order at the Chairman’s discretion. A copy of the agenda background material
provided to the Board, with the exception of items to be considered in executive session, is available for public inspection at Navajo
County website, www.navajocountyaz.gov 
9:00 a.m. Call To Order: Pledge of Allegiance and Invocation
           
1. CALL TO THE PUBLIC: Individuals may address the Board on any relevant issue for up to 5
minutes. At the close of the call to the public, Board members may not respond to any
comments but may respond to criticism, ask staff to review a matter or ask that a matter be
placed on a future agenda.
 
 
 
2. CONSENT AGENDA: These items are considered to be routine or administrative in nature and
will be approved in a single motion. A Board member may request for any item to be removed
from the consent agenda and considered on the regular agenda:
 
 
a. Minutes: November 28, 2017  
 
b. Warrant & Voucher Report over $1000, November 2017  
 
c. Clerk of Superior Court Report November 2017  
 
d. Juvenile Probation Report November 2017  
 
e. Constable Reports: Show Low Precinct #5, November; and Pinetop-Lakeside Precinct #6,
November, 2017
 
 
f. Justice Court Reports: Holbrook Precinct #1,  November; Winslow Precinct #2, November;
Snowflake Precinct #3, November; Kayenta Precinct #4, November; Show Low Precinct
#5,November; and Pinetop-Lakeside Precinct #6, November 2017 
 
 
g. Personnel Actions  
 
h. Acknowledge receipt of the complete real property Assessment Roll of Navajo County for the
Tax Year 2018 and direct the Clerk to accept it for filing, pursuant to A.R.S.§ 42-15153

<!-- page 2 -->
i. InSite Towers First Amendment to the Communications Facilities Lease and Easement  
 
j. Three (3) year lease agreement with Quality Ready Mix for office space for Major Crimes
Apprehension Team, South unit in the amount of $36,000
 
 
3. BOARD OF SUPERVISORS:  
 
a. Presentation to retiring employee, Chief Deputy James Molesa.  
Presented By: Eric Scott/Sheriff Clark 
 
b. Consideration of a Professional Services Contract between Navajo County and Brenda Clark
beginning December 1, 2017, for consultant resources to support the Navajo County Sheriff's
Office civil deputies with the recent vacancy within that division.
 
Presented By: Brandt Clark
 
c. Consideration to update the precinct boundary between Justice of the Peace Precinct 5 &
Precinct 6. 
 
Presented By: Bryan Layton
 
d. Consideration of an Intergovernmental Facilities Lease Agreement between the Town of
Snowflake and Navajo County for the lease of the Town's building located at 145 South Main
Street in Snowflake. 
 
Presented By: Bryan Layton
 
4. ADMINISTRATIVE SERVICES:  
 
a. Presentation from Steve Best, USFS Forest Supervisor on prescribed burning activities on the
Apache-Sitgreaves National Forest, Gene Beaudoin, Regional Forest Manager for State Forestry
and Fire on forest thinning projects.
 
Presented By: Mary Springer, Steve Best & Gene Beaudoin
 
b. Consideration and approval of Continuity of Operations Plan Update (COOP).  
Presented By: Catrina Jenkins/Mary Springer
 
c. Consideration and approval of the Navajo County Emergency Operations Plan Update (EOP).  
Presented By: Mary Springer/Catrina Jenkins
 
5. NAVAJO COUNTY PUBLIC HEALTH SERVICES DISTRICT: Board of Directors
Session: 
 
 
a. REGULAR AGENDA:  
 
1. Consideration of the contract between Navajo County Public Health Services District and Mare
Insights for the completion of the Navajo County Community Health Assessment and
Community Health Improvement Plan. 
 
Presented By: Jeff Lee

<!-- page 3 -->
6. PUBLIC WORKS:  
 
a. Consideration of the purchase of a 2017 New Caterpillar CB66B Asphalt Compactor,Double
Drum from Empire Machinery in the amount of $187,886.56.
 
Presented By: Jeanine Carruthers 
 
7. COUNTY BUSINESS UPDATE: Report from Board members, County Attorney and Assistant
County Manager.
 
 
 
8. ADJOURN  
 
Dated: December 28, 2017 NAVAJO COUNTY BOARD OF SUPERVISORS 
Posted: 12:15 P.M. By: MB                         
Melissa W. Buckley - Clerk of the Board

Board of Supervisors Regular 2.h.        
Meeting Date: 12/12/2017  
Title: Assessment Roll of Navajo County for the tax year 2018
Submitted By: Donna Hurstrom, Deputy Clerk of the Board
Department: Board of Supervisors
Motion before the Board:
Acknowledge receipt of the complete real property Assessment Roll of Navajo County for the
Tax Year 2018 and direct the Clerk to accept it for filing, pursuant to A.R.S.§ 42-15153
Background:
42-15153. Completion and delivery of property lists and assessment roll; use of lists by
administrative appeals bodies A. On or before December 20 of each year, the county assessor
shall complete the assessment roll and attach the assessor's certificate to the roll with a
cross-index of all property listed in the roll, showing the ownership of the property and all
assessment lists from which the roll was compiled. B. The assessor shall deliver the lists and
certified roll to the clerk of the board of supervisors who shall file them in the clerk's office. C.
The county board of equalization and the state board of equalization may use the lists for any
lawful purpose.

RE: Certification 
Melissa,
The Navajo County Assessor’s Office has completed the assessment of all parcels of real estate in 
Navajo County.
Detailed information, ownership, full cash values and supporting data are contained in the files of 
this office.  These records and the information thereon were prepared by the employees of the 
Navajo County Assessor’s Office and are available to the Board of Supervisors and the public 
upon request, in accordance with A.R.S. §42-15153.
I hereby certify that, to the best of my information and belief, the above-cited information 
represents the complete real property assessment roll of Navajo County for the tax year 2018.  I 
hereby transfer control of the assessment roll, in the Assessor’s office to the Board of Supervisors 
in accordance with A.R.S. §42-15153. 
We will continue to crosscheck the information on the assessment roll.  If any discrepancies, 
omissions, or modifications are discovered, if any changes are necessary resulting from court 
cases or other factors said changes will be made to the roll.

FIRST AME NDMENT TO COMMU NICATIONS FACILITIE S LE ASE  AND E ASEMENT
This First Amendment to Communications Facilities Lease and Easement (“First Amendment”) is made this 
____ day of ______________, 2017 (“Execution Date”), by and between InSite Towers Development, LLC , a
Delaware limited liability company (“LESSOR”), and Navajo County, a political subdivision of the State of Arizona
(“TENANT”).
WHE REAS,LESSOR, as successor in interest to Genesis PMCS, LLC, and Don Letcher d/b/a Mountain 
Top Communications, and TENANT are parties to a certain Communications Facilities Lease and Easement dated 
October 15, 2010 (the “Lease”) for a portion of LESSOR’s telecommunications site located 34° - 12' - 20.80" N, 109° 
- 56' - 33.20" W at Forest Service Road 225, Lakeside, County of Navajo AZ 85929 (known to LESSOR as “AZ020 
Porter Mountain”) and
WHE REAS, LESSOR and TENANT, in their mutual interest, wish to amend the Lease as set forth below 
accordingly.
NOW THE REFORE, in consideration of the foregoing and other good and valuable consideration, the 
receipt and sufficiency of which are hereby acknowledged, LESSOR and TENANT agree as follows:
1.  Any capitalized term used herein, but not defined, shall have the meaning ascribed to such term in the Lease.
2. The term of the Lease shall be extended to provide that the Lease has a new initial term of five (5) years (“New 
Initial Term”) which commenced on December 1, 2017 (“Amendment Commencement Date”).  The Lease will 
automatically renew, commencing on the expiration of the New Initial Term, for up to four (4) separate consecuti ve 
additional periods of five (5) years each (each such five (5) year additional period is hereinafter referred to as an 
“Additional Extension Term”), upon the same terms and conditions of the Lease, as amended herein, unless TENANT
notifies LESSOR in writing of TENANT’s intention not to renew the Lease at least ninety (90) days prior to the 
expiration of the New Initial Term or the then current Additional Extension Term. The New Initial Term and the 
Additional Extension Term(s) are collectively referred to as the Term (“Term”).
3. As of the Amendment Commencement Date, the current monthly rent payable under the Lease shall be increased 
by One Thousand Six Hundred and 00/100 Dollars ($1,600.00) per month to Two Thousand Four Hundred and 00/100 
Dollars ($2,400.00) per month.  On each anniversary of the Amendment Commencement Date during the Term, the 
monthly rent shall increase by three percent (3%) over the monthly rent in effect immediately prior to such increase.
As of the Amendment Commencement Date, Section 5 shall be deleted in its entirety. 
4. As of the Amendment Commencement Date, Tenant Facilities and Premises, depicted in Exhibits 1 and 2 of the Lease, 
shall be deleted in their entirety, and replaced with the Exhibit attached hereto and incorporated herein by reference.
5. Notwithstanding any provision contained in this Lease to the contrary, following the expiration of the New Initial 
Term, TENANT may, terminate this Lease for economic reasons, without further liability upon ninety (90) days prior 
written notice to LESSOR.  If TENANT elects to terminate this Lease, then TENANT will pay to LESSOR a 
termination fee in the amount of twelve (12) months of the then current monthly rent (“Termination Fee”).
6.  LESSOR’s notice addresses in Section 16 of the Lease are hereby deleted in their entirety and replaced with the 
following:

Board of Supervisors Regular 2.j.        
Meeting Date: 12/12/2017  
Title: Quality Ready Mix Lease
Submitted For: KC Clark, Sheriff  Submitted By: Cheryl Powell, Deputy Chief 
Department: Sheriff 
Motion before the Board:
Three (3) year lease agreement with Quality Ready Mix for office space for Major Crimes
Apprehension Team, South unit in the amount of $36,000
Background:
The Major Crimes Apprehension Team (MCAT) has been leasing office space for their South
County unit since 2013.  Funding has once again been approved by HIDTA to lease the space in a
building owned by Brian Brimhall of Quality Ready Mix.   It is a three year lease for $36,000
however Mr. Brimhall will accept the annual lease amount of $12,000.
It is expensed from HIDTA fund 220397.
Fiscal Impact
Fiscal Year: FY17
Budgeted Y/N: Y
Amount Requested: 12,000
Fiscal Impact:
Paid from HIDTA grant funds 220397.037.819

Page1
LEASE AGREEMENT
This Lease Agreement ("Agreement"), made this _ _ _ _ _ _       d    a    y ,     o f_________, 2017 by and
between Quality Redi­Mix (hereinafter referred to as "Landlord"), and  N avajo  C o unty 
S herif f’ s Dep art me n t(hereinafter referred to as "Tenant"). Both the Tenant and the
Landlord shall be collectively referred to as the Parties. 
WITNESSETH, that in consideration of the rents and covenants hereinafter set forth, 
Landlord hereby leases to Tenant and Tenant hereby rents from Landlord approximately1,500
square feet of office space home, located at 1250 Penrod Lane, Show Low, Arizona 85901
(collectively referred to as the "Premises" or the "Leased Premises"), upon the following terms and
conditions:
SECTION 1. This Lease Agreement shall remain in effect from the date of the signing of the 
agreement for a thirty­six (36) month period. This agreement is renewable for an additional thirty­
six (36) month period, contingent uponfunding from the Federal Government becoming available for 
the Tenant, and as agreed by both parties. This agreement is also terminable in accordance with the 
provisions of A.R.S. § 38­511, and that statute is hereby incorporated into this agreement by 
reference.
SECTION 2. Tenant covenants and agrees to pay to Landlord one thousand dollars 
($1,000.00) per month to the Landlord, submitted to B r a in B r imha ll, Q ua lity R e di­ Mix, as
rental for the leased premises. Payment for the entire thirty­six (36) month period of this 
Agreement shall be tenured to Brian Brimhall at one time in the amount of thirty­six thousand dollars 
($36,000.00). It is understood and agreed that if Tenant shall abandon, vacate, or surrender the
Leased Premises or be dispossessed by process of law, the Landlord and/or Brian Brimhall 
shall reimburse the Tenant one thousand dollars ($1,000.00) per month for each month remaining in 
the thirty­six (36) month period of this Agreement. 
SECTION 3. It is understood and agreed tha t Landlord shall not make any
improvements to the Premises, and any improvements or alterations made or any signs placed upon
the leased Premises by Tenant shall be subject to Landlord's prior written approval. Tenant agrees
to accept leased space on an "as is" basis. Notwithstanding the foregoing, Landlord warrants that as
of the commencement date of this Agreement, that HVAC, electric, plumbing, interior walls, flooring,
and all windows are in satisfactory condition and functioning. Any HVAC, repairs, improvements
or alterations will be undertaken at Landlord's sole expense. Landlord shall, at Landlord’s sole 
expense, maintain the exterior, roof, and structural portions of the building on the leased premises, 
windows and doors, equipment and appurtenances thereof (including lighting, heating, air conditioning, 
and plumbing fixtures) in a good state of repair, reasonable wear and tear excepted.  At the time of 
termination of this Lease by either party, Landlord will inspect the building.  Tenant will be responsible 
for any damages caused by Tenant other than ordinary wear and tear.
SECTION 4. The Leased Premises shall be used by Tenant solely for the purpose of 
operating an office. Tenant covenants and agrees to perform all business on the Leased 
Premises in a dignified manner and to maintain the Leased Premises in a clean and orderly 
condition; failing to do so at Landlord's sole discretion shall give Landlord the right to terminate 
this Lease Agreement if Tenant shall not have commenced compliance with the written or oral

<!-- page 401 -->
Page2
notice of Landlord within 48 hours after receipt thereof. 
SECTION 5. Landlord agrees the one thousand dollars ($1,000.00) monthly payment 
includes payment for all utilities, including electric, natural gas, trash, and water. Landlord shall be
responsi ble for all utilities. 
SECTION 6. When Tenant vacates space, they will be responsible for leaving the property in 
the same condition as when it was originally leased.
SECTION 7. Tenant agrees that it will repair promptly at its own expense any damage to the
Leased Premises caused by bringing into the Leased Premises any property for Tenant's use, or by
the installation or removal of such property, or by the negligence of Tenant or Tenant's employees,
and that it will surrender the Leased Premises at the expiration of the term hereon in as good
condition as when received, excepting depreciation caused by ordinary wear and tear, and damage
by fire, unavoidable accident, or act of God.
SECTION 8.In addition to the Leased Premises, Tenant shall have the right of
non­exclusive use in common with others; of automobile parking areas, driveways, footways
and such other facilities as may be designated by Landlord, subject to reasonable rules and 
regulations for the use thereof as prescribed by Landlord.
SECTION 9. Landlord covenants that Tenant, upon performance of the terms herein, 
shall peaceably and quietly hold and enjoy the Leased Premises during the term or renewal of this
Lease without hindrance or interruption by Landlord or any other person.
SECTION 10. To the extent permitted by law, Landlord and Tenant agree to indemnify, 
defend and hold each other harmless from and against injury, loss, damage or liability (or any claims
in respect of the foregoing), costs or expenses (including reasonable attorneys' fees and court costs)
arising from the use or occupancy of the Premises/Property, or any other activity on or about the
Premises/Property, or for the breach of this Agreement, to the extent attributable to the negligence or
intentional act or omission of the indemnifying party, its employees, agents or independent contractors.
This Section shall survive termination of this Agreement.
SECTION 11. Tenant covenants and agrees that Tenant will carry and maintain, at its 
sole cost and expense, the following types of insurance, in the amount specified and in the form 
hereinafter provided for: Bodily injury liability insurance with limits of not less than Five Hundred
Thousand Dollars ($500,000.00) per person and One Million Dollars ($1,000,000.00) per
occurrence insuring against any and all liability of the insured with respect to said Premises or
arising out of the maintenance, use or occupancy thereof, and property damage liability insurance 
with a limit of not less than One Hundred Thousand Dollars ($100,000.00) per accident or
occurrence. All such bodily injury liability insurance and property damage liability insurance shall
specifically insure the performance by Tenantof the indemnity as to liability for injury to or the
death of persons and injury or damage of property herein contained.
SECTION 12. Tenant cannot transfer, assign this Agreement in conjunction with the sale of 
Tenant's business or the sale or transfer by Tenants of all or substantially all of its assets.
Further, Tenant cannot sublease the Leased Premises to any of its independent Agents.

<!-- page 402 -->
Page3
SECTION 13. Landlord reserves the right to enter upon the rented premises at 
reasonable times for the purpose of inspecting the premises. Reasonable times shall be 
considered, at a minimum, of 24­hour notice.
SECTION 14. Tenant hereby agrees to abide by all federal, state, county, and municipal 
rules and written regulations as may be promulgated from time to time by Landlord.
SECTION 15. The failure of Tenant to fully perform under any or all of the terms and 
conditions of this agreement shall constitute a Breach of this agreement, entitling the Landlord to 
take any and all such action provided by law including, but not limited to, one or more of the 
following: (1) Enter the premises and remove all persons and property there from, (2) Declare 
the agreement at an end and terminated.
SECTION 16. Any breach alleged under this agreement, shall be occasioned by a twenty 
(20) days written notice of the same to the defaulting party. If at the end of such twenty (20) days as
provided in said notice, the defaulting party has not cured the breach, the offending party may take
any and all such action required by law.
SECTION 17. Tenant shall not vacate or abandon the Leased Premises at any time 
during the Term; and if Tenant shall abandon, vacate, or surrender the Leased Premises or be 
dispossessed by process of law, or otherwise, any property belonging to Tenant and left on the
Leased Premises shall be returned to Navajo County, as public property, and Navajo County shall 
deal with the property according to Navajo County policies and state statutes. 
SECTION 18. Upon reasonable notice from Tenant, Landlord shall make necessary 
structural repair to the roof, foundation, exterior walls and any load­bearing interior walls of the 
Demised Premises. Landlord shall not be required to repair any damage caused by any negligent 
acts, or omissions of Tenant, Tenant's Agents or Tenant's invitees. 
SECTION 19. Any holding over after the expiration of the Term, with the consent of 
Landlord, shall be construed to be a tenancy from month­to­month, cancellable upon thirty (30) 
days' written notice, and a rental and upon terms and conditions as existing during the last year 
of the Term.
SECTION 20. If any term, covenant, condition, or provision of this Lease is held by a 
court of competent jurisdiction to be void or unenforceable, the remainder of the provisions of 
this Lease shall remain in full force and shall in no way be affected, impaired, or invalidated.
SECTION 21. If a condemnation or a transfer in lieu thereof occurs on all or any portion 
of the Leased Premises, Landlord or Tenant may, upon written notice given within thirty (30) 
days after the taking or transfer, terminate this Lease. Tenant shall not be entitled to share in any 
portion of the award, and Tenant expressly waives any right or claim to any part of the award. 
Tenant shall, however, have the right to claim and recover, only from the condemning authority, 
any amounts necessary to reimburse Tenant for the cost of removing stock and fixtures. Landlord 
shall not be entitled to continue to receive the one thousand dollars ($1,000.00) monthly 
payments. Additionally, any remaining rental funds being held in the escrow account shall be 
immediately tenured to the Navajo County Sheriff’s office.

<!-- page 403 -->
Page4
SECTION 22. This Lease contains the entire understanding of the parties with respect to 
the subject matter hereof and supersedes any and all other oral or written agreements or 
understandings between the Parties. Neither Party has made or relied on any promise, 
understanding, warranty or representation other than as specifically set forth herein. This
Lease may not be changed, modified or amended except by writteninstrument signed by both
parties hereto. This Lease supersedes any prior agreement between the parties.
SECTION 23. If any provision of this Lease, as applied to any party or to any 
circumstances, is adjudged by a court to be invalid or unenforceable, the same will in no way
affect any other provision hereof, the application of such provision in any other circumstances, or 
the validity or enforceability of this Lease. In such event the parties shall agree on a mutually 
acceptable substitute provision.
SECTION 24. The parties expressly agree that this Agreement was jointly drafted, and 
that both had opportunity to negotiate its terms and to obtain the assistance of counsel in 
reviewing its terms and to obtain the assistance of counsel in reviewing its terms prior to 
execution. Therefore, this Agreement shall be constructed neither against nor in favor of either 
party, but shall be constructed in a neutral manner.
SECTION 25. In the event that litigation . results from or arises out of this Agreement  or 
the performance thereof,  the Parties  agree to reimburse  the prevailing party's  reasonable 
attorney's fees, court costs, and all other expenses, whether or not taxable by the court as costs, in 
addition to any other relief to which the prevailing party may be entitled.
SECTION 26. Nothing in this Agreement shall be deemed to constitute a partnership or 
joint venture between the Parties, nor shall either Party be deemed the agent of the other. This 
Agreement shall not be construed to create rights, expressed or implied, on behalf of, or for the 
use of, any Parties, aside from LANDLORD and TENANT and shall not be obligated, separately 
or jointly, to any third parties or any third party beneficiaries by virtue of this Agreement.
SECTION 27. In the event of damage to the Leased Premises resulting from fire, 
flooding, lightning, storm, earthquake, explosions, public enemy or other similar unavoidable 
accident or casualty not the fault of the TENANT, and if, as a result of any of the aforesaid

then this Lease, as it pertains to the particular Lease Premises damaged or destroyed, may be

SECTION 28. If any portion of this Agreement is found to be unenforceable by a court of 
competent jurisdiction, the remaining terms and provisions unaffected thereby shall remain in full 
force and effect.
SECTION 29. In the event of a disagreement between the parties regarding the 
interpretation of this Agreement or their respective obligations under this Agreement, the parties 
agree that they will first make efforts to resolve the matter through alternative dispute resolution 
prior to proceeding to litigation in Court. In the event of a dispute that the parties are unable to

CONTRACT FOR PROFESSIONAL SERVICES
Agreement made as of December 1. 2017, between Navajo County (“County”) and Brenda Clark
(“Consultant”).
Whereas, County requires certain consultant resources with unique knowledge and expertise to 
perform services to support the Navajo County Sheriff’s Office civil deputies with the recent vacancy 
within that division.
Whereas, Consultant represents that it has the necessary expertise and unique knowledge to provide 
such services in accordance with the terms of this Agreement.
Now, Therefore, the parties agree as follows:
1. Services.  Consultant shall provide administrative support services and training oversight to the 
Navajo County Sheriff’s Office civil deputies division in a competent and professional manner to 
the satisfaction of the County.  
2. Consultant’s Expertise.  Consultant warrants that it has the ability, authority, capacity and 
professional expertise to perform this agreement.  Consultant shall self-perform this agreement 
and be devoted to the successful accomplishment of the work to be performed hereunder.  Once 
work commences on this agreement, consultant shall adhere to agreed upon timeline and 
commitment to successful completion of the contracted services.
3. Independent Contractor.  Consultant acknowledges that it is an independent contractor. Neither 
party is or shall be an agent, employee, partner, joint venturer or associate of the other.  An 
employee or agent of one party shall not be deemed an employer or agent of the other party for 
any purpose.  Taxes or Social Security contributions will not be withheld from any payment by the 
County, and Consultant shall be solely responsible for such matters.
4. Subcontracts. Consultant shall not enter into any subcontract with respect to any of the work to 
be performed hereunder without the County’s prior written approval.  All subcontracts shall comply 
with applicable federal and state laws and regulations and shall impose on the subcontractor 
substantially the same obligations as are imposed on Consultant by this Agreement with respect 
to those matters covered by Sections 8, 9, 10, 12, 15 and 18.  Consultant is responsible for full 
performance of this Agreement regardless of whether subcontractors are used.
5. Time for Completion.  Consultant’s services shall be performed in accordance with the agreed 
upon schedule for the performance of the Services hereunder.  Time limits established by a 
schedule approved by the County shall not, except for reasonable cause, be exceeded by County 
or Consultant.  The schedule shall be adjusted, if necessary, as the Project proceeds.  
6. Payment. County shall pay Consultant a monthly rate of $3,000.00 per month for services 
rendered by Consultant pursuant to this agreement. The County shall reimburse the Consultant 
in state travel expenses in accordance with the Navajo County travel policy reimbursement rates.
Out of state travel arrangements shall be mutually agreed upon between both parties prior to 
travel.  Consultant shall be authorized to drive and have access to the use of Navajo County

<!-- page 409 -->
vehicles while performing services according to this agreement. Payments shall be due and 
payable upon receipt of Consultant’s monthly invoice.  
7. Defects in Work. County may reject any work product that fails to meet customary professional 
standards or Project specifications.  Consultant agrees to promptly remedy all such deficiencies.  
The parties shall make a good faith effort to resolve any controversy or claim through informal 
negotiation as set forth in Section 12.  No compensation shall be paid for any rejected work until 
such issues have been resolved.
8. Indemnity.  To the fullest extent allowable by law, Consultant shall defend, indemnify and hold 
harmless County and its representatives, officers, officials and employees (the “Indemnitees”) 
from and against all claims, damages, losses and expenses (including but not limited to attorneys 
fees, court costs, and the costs of appellate proceedings) relating to, arising out of or resulting 
from Consultant’s negligent acts, errors, mistakes or omissions in the performance of this 
Agreement.  Consultant’s duty to defend, hold harmless and indemnify the Indemnitees shall arise 
in connection with any claim, damage, loss or expense that is attributable to bodily injury, 
sickness, disease, death or injury to, impairment or destruction of property including loss of use 
resulting therefrom cause whole or in part by any negligent act, error, mistake or omission in the 
performance of this Agreement (including those by any person for whose negligent acts, errors, 
mistakes or omissions Consultant may be liable) to the extent of Consultant’s negligence or fault.  
The amount and type of insurance coverage requirements set forth above shall not limit the scope 
of the indemnity in this paragraph.
9. Records. Consultant shall retain, and shall require each subcontractor (if applicable) to retain, all 
books, accounts, reports, files and other records relating to this Agreement for a period of five 
years after completion of the work.  All such documents shall be subject to inspection and audit by 
County upon reasonable notice during normal business hours.  A legible copy of any or all such 
documents shall be produced by Consultant at the request and expense of County.
10.Non-Assignment. Consultant shall not assign any right in this Agreement without County’s prior 
written approval, nor shall Consultant delegate any duty hereunder without County’s prior written 
approval.
11.Negotiation of Disputes. The parties shall make a good faith effort to resolve any claim or 
controversy or claim through informal negotiation.  Notice of any claim or controversy shall be 
provided in writing, with supporting documentation, to the recipient designated in Section 21.  The 
recipient shall have seven calendar days to prepare and deliver a written response.  If the parties 
fail to resolve the disputes within a reasonable period of not less than ten days, either party may 
pursue available legal remedies.
12.Suspension and Termination by County:
a. Suspension.  County may, without cause, order Consultant in writing to suspend, delay or 
interrupt its performance in whole or part.  An adjustment shall be made to the completion date 
and for any increase in cost of performance resulting from the suspension, delay or 
interruption.  No adjustment shall be made to the extent that performance is or would have 
been suspended, delayed or interrupted by another cause for which Consultant is responsible.
b. Termination for Cause.  County may terminate this Agreement for cause if Consultant 
refuses or fails to perform this Agreement; disregards laws, ordinances, rules, regulations or 
orders of a public authority having jurisdiction; or otherwise commits a material breach of this 
Agreement. When any of the foregoing causes exist, County shall give Consultant written

<!-- page 410 -->
notice and a  ten-day opportunity to cure the default.  If the default remains uncured, County 
may (without prejudice to any rights or remedies it may have) terminate this agreement and 
finish the work or cause it to be finished.  If the unpaid balance of the Agreement exceeds the 
cost of finishing the work (including expenses incurred by the County as a result of the 
Consultant’s default), Consultant shall be entitled to payment for its performance up to the 
amount of such excess.  If the cost of finishing the work exceeds the unpaid balance, 
Consultant shall pay the difference to County.
c. Termination for Convenience. County, by written notice to Consultant, may terminate this 
agreement in whole or part when in the sole discretion of County it is in County’s best interests 
to do so.  Consultant shall be paid for all services provided.
d. Consultant’s Responsibility.  Upon receipt of a termination notice, Consultant shall (a) 
promptly discontinue all services (unless notice directs otherwise), and (b) deliver or otherwise 
make available to County copies of all data, reports, summaries and other information and 
materials developed or accumulated by Consultant in performing this Agreement.
13.Termination by Consultant.  Consultant may terminate this agreement upon written notice to 
County if performance is made impossible for a period of thirty (30) consecutive calendar days for 
any of the following reasons through no act or fault of Consultant or its agents, employees or 
subcontractors:  issuance of an order by a count or other public authority having jurisdiction; an 
act of government, such as a declaration of national emergency; or a natural disaster or other Act 
of God.  Consultant may also terminate this agreement upon written notice and a ten-day 
opportunity to cure if County fails to make any payment within time set forth in Section 6.  
Consultant may also terminate this agreement upon written notice if suspensions, delays or 
interruptions by County equal in the aggregate more than 100% of the total number of days 
scheduled for completion.
14.Governing Law.  This agreement shall be governed by the law of the State of Arizona.  Any suit 
arising out of this agreement shall be brought in the state courts of Arizona (with venue in Navajo 
County) or federal District of Arizona, but only after informal negotiation pursuant to Section 12.
15.Incorporation of Applicable Laws. Every provision of law required by statute or regulation to be 
in this Agreement will be read and enforced as though included herein.  Each party shall promptly 
notify the other upon discovery that any such provision has been omitted.
16.Non-Collusion.  The provisions A.R.S. 38-511 are incorporated herein by this reference.
17.One-Year Limitation on Actions.  No action shall be maintained by Consultant on any claim 
based upon or arising out of this agreement unless such action is commenced within one year 
after the County’s final payment hereunder.
18.Term. This Contract for Professional Service is valid for six (6) months from the date the 
agreement was made between the two parties. This contract shall be renewed for an additional six 
(6) month term, unless either party provides thirty (30) days written notice, prior to the expiration of 
the agreement, of their intent to not renew the agreement. 
19.Entire Agreement. This agreement and those documents incorporated by reference represent 
the entire understanding between the parties.  No amendment shall be effective unless executed 
by both parties with the same formality as this agreement.  Provided, however, that the County’s 
designated representative is authorized to modify the Scope of Work, in writing, with the

<!-- page 411 -->
concurrence of Consultant so long as the project is not changed substantially or significant 
additional compensation is not required.
20.Severability. The provisions of this agreement are severable.  Any provision held to be invalid or 
unenforceable shall not affect the validity or enforceability of any other provision.
21.Notices. All notices, invoices and payments shall be in writing and may be given by personal or 
mail delivery. The designated recipients are as follows:

Motion before the Board:
Consideration to update the precinct boundary between Justice of the Peace Precinct 5 & Precinct
6. 
Background:
The City of Show Low has experienced commercial/retail growth on White Mountain Road and
Scott Ranch Road which includes retailers such as Walmart, Home Depot, and Lowes.  The Show
Low Police Department (SLPD) has law enforcement jurisdiction of this area, but cases are cited
in Precinct 6 which requires SLPD Officers to travel to the Justice Court in Pinetop/Lakeside.

boundaries.

Prepared by Navajo County Page 1 of 2 October 2017
Navajo County - For Official Use Only
Proposal to Adjust Precinct Boundaries of Justice of the Peace Precinct 5 & 6
Issue: The County proposes to update the precinct boundary between Precinct 5 and Precinct 6 to more 
closely reflect the existing city limits of Show Low to include the commercial/retail development on White 
Mountain Road and Scott Ranch Road.  
Background: The City of Show Low has experienced commercial/retail growth on White Mountain Road 
north of Scott Ranch Road which includes retailers such as Walmart, Home Depot, and Lowes. The Show 
Low Police Department (SLPD) has law enforcement jurisdiction of this area, but cases are cited in Precinct 
6 which requires SLPD Officers to travel to the Justice Court in Pinetop/Lakeside.  
Impact on Public Safety: The County proposes to update the precinct boundary between Precinct 5 and 
Precinct 6 to more closely reflect the existing city limits of Show Low to include the commercial/retail 
development on White Mountain Road north of Scott Ranch Road.  This change would allow SLPD Officers 
to cite cases in the Show Low Justice Court and decrease travel times for law enforcement when needed 
to appear in court.
Impact on Residents: Most of the residential homes in unincorporated land within the vicinity are 
proposed to remain unchanged in Precinct 6.  Analysis of the area using the most recent census data 
shows that 399 residents currently in Precinct 6 would move to Precinct 5. In total, the Precinct 5 
population is projected to increase from 17,463 to 17,862, or 2.3%.  Conversely, the Precinct 6 population 
is projected to decrease from 24,283 to 23,884, or -1.6%.  As a percent of total population within the 
precincts, the estimated impact to the Native populations would be a less than 1% change in both 
precincts.
Impact on Justice Courts:Although the specific number of court cases attributed to the retail/commercial 
area on White Mountain Road north of Scott Ranch Road is not available, both judges from Precinct 5 and 
Precinct 6 estimate that the total impact will not be sufficient to change the tier size of either court based 
on Judicial Productivity Credits (JPCs).  It is expected that both courts will remain in their existing tiers and 
compensation for judges will remain unchanged.  It is also expected that the impact on Precinct 5 and 
Precinct 6 Constables will be similar and compensation for constables will remain unchanged.  
The table below provides total population impact:

Board of Supervisors Regular 5.a.1.        
Meeting Date: 12/12/2017  
Title: Contract with Mare Insights 
Submitted For: Jeffrey Lee, Health Director  Submitted By: Jeffrey Lee, Health Director
Department: NC Public Health Services District
Presented By: Jeff Lee
Motion before the Board:
Consideration of the contract between Navajo County Public Health Services District and Mare
Insights for the completion of the Navajo County Community Health Assessment and Community
Health Improvement Plan. 
Background:
Community Health Assessment and Improvement Planning
The fundamental purpose of public health is defined by three core functions: assessment, policy
development and assurance. Community health assessments (CHAs) provide information for
problem and asset identification and policy formulation, implementation, and evaluation. CHAs
also help measure how well a public health system is fulfilling its assurance function.
 A CHA should be part of an ongoing broader community health improvement process.
A community health improvement process uses CHA data to identify priority issues, develop and
implement strategies for action, and establish accountability to ensure measurable health
improvement, which are often outlined in the form of a community health improvement plan
(CHIP). A community health improvement process looks outside of the performance of an
individual organization serving a specific segment of a community to the way in which the
activities of many organizations contribute to community health improvement.
A variety of tools and processes may be used to conduct a community health improvement
process; the essential ingredients are community engagement and collaborative participation
 
Recommendation: 
Approval of the contract with Mare Insights for the completion of the NCPHSD Community
Health Assessment and Community Health Improvement Plan

accreditation by the Public Health Accreditation Board (PHAB).

Mare Insights Bid for CHA/CHIP – Page 1 
Draft Bid for NCPHSD Community Health Assessment 
And Community Health Improvement Plan 
 
This proposal is for leading the Navajo County Public Health Services District through the process of 
developing a community health assessment (CHA) and community health improvement plan (CHIP). 
These documents, and the process of creating them, are essential for building community partners, 
identifying and prioritizing key community health issues and setting a strategic direction for the 
organization.   
 
The proposal is based on best practices used by other health departments, including guidelines from the 
Public Health Accreditation Board (PHAB).  In this document, there are references to the PHAB standards 
and measures, such as “PHAB 1.2.1.1.”  The entire PHAB document is available online at: 
http://www.phaboard.org/wp-content/uploads/SM-Version-1.5-Board-adopted-FINAL-01-24-
2014.docx.pdf 
 
The proposal begins with an overview, followed by a detailed list of activities and deliverables.   
The next section includes a budget summary and detailed budget.  The appendices are examples of best 
practice processes used for CHA/CHIP development from two different sources.

Details of Activities and Deliverables 
 
The table below shows a detailed list of activities and deliverables.  
 
Detailed Activities and Deliverables 
 
Community Meetings to Plan CHA 
Two community meetings – in two different areas of county. Purpose of meetings is to plan the CHA/CHIP 
process, per PHAB 1.1.1. and 5.2.3.  These meetings will bring together community partners in healthcare, 
behavioral health, law enforcement, education, emergency management and others to help plan how the rest 
of the process will proceed.   
Deliverables: Meeting agendas/facilitation guide, meeting notes, monthly status report s. 
 
Focus Groups on Health issues  
Two focus groups - one each in two different parts of county. These groups should be among community 
members who are not necessarily part of the public health community – community members as opposed to 
community organization partners (as was appropriate for the CHA planning meetings).  Purpose is to learn 
what community members see as priorities.  Meets PHAB 1.1.2.1 for qualitative research portion of CHA. 
Deliverables: Discussion guide, focus group facilitation notes, report of findings, including comparison of two 
communities. 
 
CHA Online Survey (Optional) 
Online survey sent to various stakeholders, posts for public etc. Required for PHAB 1.1.2.1 to have quantitative 
research in CHA, but epidemiological data will meet this requirement so online survey listed as optional.  
Purpose of survey is to learn about community perceptions of health issues and priorities – goals similar to 
focus groups but done among a larger group of residents (quantitative). 
Deliverables: Survey design; programming, pilot test result; copy for email announcing online survey: 
tabulation and report including two-page key findings, delivery of Excel file with responses, topline with 
graphs, charts from online software. 
 
Community Meetings for CHA Review, Priority Setting 
Two community meetings among community organization partners to review CHA and outcomes of previous 
meetings and focus groups.  Participants will select priorities for the county and discuss possible ways to 
address.  Meeting should be carefully planned to engage partners in the process and elicit involvement from 
their organizations.  Result will be used for CHIP.  Meets PHAB 1.1.3, 1.1.2.2. 
Deliverables: Discussion guide; notes from meeting; brief report. 
 
CHA Final Report 
Compile and analyze information from focus groups, online survey, two data analyses (listed above), and 
community meetings and create a final report that meets PHAB standards. Meet PHAB 1.1.2.1 .  Gather and 
analyze epidemiological and local health system data, including the following, as available: 
• Data included in last CHA 
• Dental homes from ages 0 – 100 
• Vaccines, fully vaccinated, some, none (vaccine rate)  
• Transportation to doctors, dentist, specialist etc.  
• STD Rates 
• Mental Health issues 
• Children with Special Healthcare Needs 
• Opioid crisis  
• Substance abuse specific

<!-- page 435 -->
Mare Insights Bid for CHA/CHIP – Page 5 
• Number of physicians, specialist, specific 
• Chronic disease 
• Tobacco use 
• Out of country travelers 
• Child fatality 
• Obesity rate 
 
Deliverables: Written report of no more than 50 pages to include epidemiological data and local health system 
data and meet PHAB 1.1.2.1.   Two to four page executive summary included. Presentation to be used for CHIP 
community meeting, policy makers, public forum, web posting, etc. (optional). 
 
Public Forums to Announce CHA (and CHIP) 
 
Two public forums to announce CHA results to community partners and the public - partial fulfillment of PHAB 
1.1.3.  This is an opportunity to share what was found in the previous step – what epidemiological and 
community-based data say about the health status of the community.  Meeting can be used to create key 
messages from selected priorities (optional). 
 
Deliverables: Discussion guide and plan for meetings; short presentation to be given at meeting, created with 
NCPHSD staff; transcription of flip charts, short meeting documentation from public forums (two pages, 
bulleted) can be used for 5.2.3 (optional). 
 
CHIP Final Report 
 
CHIP final report to include achievable goals, objectives, strategies, performance measures and time frames 
per PHAB 5.2.2.  Must consolidate all information gathered in previous activities in the process.  Report should 
be user-friendly/accessible to staff, stakeholders, the public and should align with other NCPHSD strategies and 
goals. 
 
Deliverables: Electronic version of CHIP report, including table of goals, objectives, performance measures, and 
individuals/groups responsible. Transcription of flip charts, (optional) short meeting documentation from CHIP 
can be used for 5.2.3community meetings (two pages, bulleted).

Appendix A: Association for Community Health Improvement (ACHI) Assessment Process 
 
This appendix provides a brief summary of the ACHI community assessment process. 
 
Step 1: Identifying the Team and Resources 
 
Obtain support from senior leaders; determine staff team and assign roles; create work plan/timeline; 
develop budget; form assessment advisory committee; agree on advisory/decision-making roles for 
staff, advisors. 
 
Step 2:  Defining the Purpose and Scope 
 
Determine what you want to learn; define primary users/target audience for assessment results; clarify 
purpose and uses of assessment; determine geographic area and target populations. 
 
Step 3: Collecting and Analyzing Data 
 
Create data collection plan; collect primary, secondary data; evaluate data quality and validity; analyze 
data. 
 
Step 4:  Selecting Priorities 
 
Determine which values and criteria priorities will reflect; determine how many priorities are 
manageable; determine “whose priorities” will be chosen; determine when to select priorities. 
 
Step 5:  Documenting and Communicating Results 
 
Create key messages from selected priorities; prepare a written report with graphs and tables; publish 
the report on paper and electronically; community dialogue and communications plan. 
 
Step 6: Planning for Action and Monitoring Progress 
 
Define achievable goals, objectives, strategies; collect information on existing community programs and 
efforts; identify evidence about effective interventions; action plan with evaluation. 
 
 
Source for ACHI Assessment Process: http://www.assesstoolkit.org/ 
See summary of process in PDF file attached to proposal email.

WHS Scope of Work 
WILLDAN HOMELAND SOLUTIONS  
Development of 2018 Community Health Assessment 
and Community Health Improvement Plan  
Scope of Work 
1.   STATEMENT OF THE REQUIREMENT 
Navajo  County  Public  Health  Services  District  has  requested  support  from  Willdan 
Homeland  Solutions  (WHS)  for  the  revision/development  of  a  Community  Health 
Assessment (CHA) and Community Health Improvement Plan (CHIP). 
 
The fixed price contract award totals $44,875. For the complete invoicing schedule please 
refer to Appendix A: Invoicing Milestones. 
2.   RELEVANT WORK EXPERIENCE 
Willdan Homeland Solutions (WHS) will provide staff skilled in public health planning and 
well versed in the Public Health Accreditation Board (PHAB) accreditation requirements 
and the Standards & Measures Version 1.5. The proposed staff have conducted similar 
work for Greenlee County Health Department, Graham County Health Department, and 
Pinal County Public Health Services District in Arizona.  
Greenlee County 
WHS  has  worked  with  Greenlee  County  for  the  past  four  phases  of  the  Arizona 
Department  of  Health  Services  (ADHS)  Intergovernmental  Agreement  (IGA)  for  Public 
Health  Accreditation  Preparation  Activities  (4/30/2012‐3/30/2013,  10/1/2014‐
9/30/2015, 10/1/2015‐9/30/2016, and 10/1/2016‐6/30/2017).   Willdan first developed 
the  Community  Health  Assessment,  Community  Health  Improvement  Plan,  and 
Department  Strategic  Plan,  which  are  pre‐requisites  in  the  application  for  PHAB 
accreditation.  For the second contract, WHS conducted deliverables towards “Progress 
Towards Preparing or Applying for Accreditation” and “Workforce Development”.  During 
the third round of funding from ADHS towards Accreditation, WHS worked with Greenlee 
County to establish a system of Performance Management, conducting a performance 
management self‐assessment and developing a written Performance Management Plan, 
and  in  building  a  culture  of  Quality  Improvement.   Most  recently,  WHS  conducted  a 
Community Health Assessment and updated the Community Health Improvement Plan.  
WHS will use these best practices and lessons learned in the work requested by Navajo 
County.

                     Greenlee County Health Department

3.   PROPOSED ACTION 
WHS  will  provide  Navajo  County  Public  Health  Services  District  with  a  highly  qualified 
team to support the deliverables, including a Project Manager and Senior Planner, (Eliza 
Coll,  MHA,  CEM,  Director  of  Public  Health  Services),  a  data  analyst,  a  graphic 
designer/technical editor, and a billing administration/support specialist. 
Based on guidance from the Public Health Accreditation Board (PHAB), the Community 
Health  Assessment  and  County/Community  Health  Improvement  Plan  must  be 
comprehensively reviewed and updated every five years.   WHS will begin by reviewing  
the existing CHA and CHIP, and identifying areas for update as well as sources for primary 
and secondary data, as well as qualitative and quantitative data to document the health 
of Navajo County.    
 
WHS will convene a CHA/CHIP Working Group to guide the development of the plans and 
to provide feedback throughout the course of the project.  Additionally, to gather primary 
data and qualitative data, WHS will engage county and local stakeholders and the general

<!-- page 448 -->

WHS Scope of Work 
public to create end products that meet and/or exceeds the criteria set forth in the PHAB 
guidance.   WHS  has  used  strategies  including  surveys,  public  forums,  stakeholder 
meetings, and other means to gather the level of feedback and involvement needed to 
complete  the  CHA  and  CHIP.   WHS  support  will  include  meetings  with  county 
stakeholders,  document  development,  and  project  reports.  Specific  tasks  that  will  be 
accomplished by WHS are listed below: 
 
Planning Support by WHS 
 Coordinate and facilitate up to (4) in‐person meetings with Navajo County to meet 
with the CHA/CHIP Working Group, stakeholders, and/or members of the public.  
Additional ad 
hoc meetings may take place by phone or in person as agreed upon;  
 WHS will develop all meeting documents, including meeting minutes;

 WHS will develop and provide a final Community Health Assessment for approval

 WHS will develop and provide a final Community Health Improvement Plan for

Planning Support by NCPHSD 
 Navajo  County  will  provide  all  necessary  plans  and  policies  related  to  the 
development of the CHA and CHIP upon contract award, for review and discovery 
as appropriate; 
 Navajo County will identify a primary Point of Contact throughout the project; 
 Navajo
 County will identify internal and external stakeholders to participate in the 
planning process and provide contact information as requested;  
 Navajo County will review and provide feedback on draft plans as requested in a 
timely manner, and provide approval for final plans  
 
4.   PLANNING TIMELINE 
WHS is able to meet or exceed the proposed schedule, beginning work in October 2017 
and completing all deliverables by September 2018.   
 
Upon contract award, WHS Project Manager, Eliza Coll, will conduct a Project Kick Off 
meeting with Navajo County Public Health Services District to confirm project scope and 
planning timeline.

1		
	PROJECT	OVERVIEW:		Coordinated	Consulting	Services	(CCS),	LLC,	is	pleased	to	submit	a	proposal	to	update	both	the	2012	Navajo	County	Community	Health	Status	Assessment,	herein	referred	to	as	the	Community	Health	Assessment	(CHA),	as	well	as	the	Navajo	County	Community	Health	Improvement	Plan	(CHIP).		CCS	intends	to	update	the	Navajo	County	CHA	and	CHIP	so	that	the	reports	reflect	the	current	needs	and	gaps	of	the	population	of	Navajo	County,	as	well	as	the	evidence-based	practices,	programs	and	policies	recommended	by	the	National	Association	of	County	and	City	Health	Officials	(NACCHO),	the	Public	Health	Accreditation	Board	(PHAB),	the	Robert	Wood	Johnson	Foundation,	the	Centers	for	Disease	Control	and	Prevention	(CDC),	the	American	Public	Health	Association	(APHA),	the	Arizona	Public	Health	Association	(AZPHA)	and	academic	literature.	The	updated	CHA	will	include	the	review	of	the	current	data	sources	of	Navajo	County	and	the	State	of	Arizona.	The	CHIP	will	include	specific,	measurable,	attainable,	relevant	and	time	sensitive	(SMART)	goals,	measurable	outcomes	and	actionable	strategies	for	the	identified	community	needs	highlighted	in	the	CHA.	Each	of	these	goals	will	leverage	existing	and	new	partnerships,	programs	and	resources.

<!-- page 453 -->
2		
	PROJECT	DELIVERABLES:		1)	In-person	Site	Visits/Planning	Meetings:	(throughout	duration	of	project)	CCS	will	facilitate	four,	quarterly,	in-person	meetings	to	kick	off	the	project,	develop	an	engaged	Navajo	County	Planning	Team	who	will	participate	in	the	review	and	development	of	a	newly	revised	Navajo	County	CHA	and	CHIP	and	meet	with	other	Navajo	County	Health	Staff	and	partners	who	will	be	instrumental	in	the	MAPP	process.	CCS	will	also	participate	in	other	Navajo	County	subject	specific	planning	groups	that	support	the	CHA	and	CHIP	process,	as	able.		CCS	anticipates	the	first	meetings	will	require	2	days	in	order	to	conduct	a	longer	initial	meeting	and	meet	with	key	partners	around	the	county.	The	other	three	meetings	will	be	1	day	for	the	planning	meeting	and	any	additional	partners.		2)	Webinar	Planning	Meetings	(throughout	the	duration	of	the	project)	During	the	other	eight	months	of	the	year,	CCS	will	plan	and	conduct	eight	webinar	meetings	for	the	Navajo	County	Planning	Team	and	partners.	CCS	will	develop	all	meeting	materials	and	presentations	and	provide	webinar	technology.		3)	Data	review	and	update	for	CHA:	(6-8	weeks)	CCS	will	conduct	a	focused	environmental	scan	and	review	the	most	current	data	available	for	the	11	category	areas	outlined	in	the	Navajo	County	Community	Health	Status	Assessment	2012	Update	and	review	up	to	16	new	category	areas	that	have	emerged	as	topics	of	interest.	This	review	requires	CCS	to	review	at	least	15	current	data	sources	and	an	additional	16-20	new	data	sources.	CCS	will	work	with	the	Navajo	County	Public	Health	Epidemiologist	to	acquire	the	majority	of	the	data,	as	well	as	the	Navajo	County	Planning	Team	to	acquire	additional	data	through	local,	regional	and	state	partners.	In	addition	to	reviewing	the	quantitative	data,	CCS	will	assist	in	the	collection	of	qualitative	data	for	the	CHA	and	CHIP	through	community	surveys,	focus	groups	and/or	key	informational	interviews.	The	analysis	of	the	current	data	sources	and	additional	data	sources,	as	well	as	the	development	of	additional	data	collection	tools	will	require	approximately	6-8	weeks.	*CCS	assumes	that	the	Navajo	County	Public	Health	Department	will	have	or	develop	data	agreements	for	all	data	sources	for	the	CHA	Current	data	sources	include:	• Census	Data/American	Community	Survey	• Bureau	of	Labor	Data

<!-- page 454 -->
3		
• Economic	Data	• Arizona	Employment	and	Population	Statistics	• Vital	Statistics	• Head	Start/Child	care	data	• Kids	Count	Data		• CDC-Immunization/Communicable	diseases	• National	Cancer	Data	• BRFSS/	Arizona	Youth	Survey	• Arizona	Department	of	Transportation	Data	• Arizona	Department	of	Health	Services	• Hospital	Data	• Doctor	Data/Encounter	Data	• Hospice	Data		New	data	Reviews	may	include:	• Dental	Homes	for	all	populations		• Vaccine	rates	• Transportation	to	doctors,	dentist,	specialist	etc.	• STD	Rates	• Mental	Health	issues	• Children	with	Special	Healthcare	Needs	• Opioid	crisis	• Substance	abuse	specific	• Number	of	physicians,	specialist,	specific	• Chronic	disease	• Tobacco	use	• Out	of	country	travelers	• Child	fatality	• Obese	rate		4)	Draft	CHA:	(4-6	weeks)		CCS	will	identify	the	needs	of	Navajo	County	through	a	comprehensive	review	of	current	and	new	quantitative	data	sources,	qualitative	data,	input	from	the	Navajo	County	Planning	Team	and	any	other	relevant	data.	The	CHA	Report	will	include	text,	tables	and	quotes	from	surveys	and/or	interviews.	Once	a	draft	CHA	is	developed	an	arduous	review	process	will	be	developed	for	the	Navajo	County	Planning	Team	and	other	Navajo	County	Public	Health	partners	to	provide	additional	input	and/or	revisions.		5)	Develop	Final	CHA:	(2-4	weeks)

<!-- page 455 -->
4		
CCS	will	develop	the	final	CHA	after	they	have	collected	all	input	from	the	draft	CHA.	The	final	CHA	report	will	be	delivered	electronically	in	both	MS	Word	and	PDF	formats.	It	will	be	provided	in	a	format	that	Navajo	County	Public	Health	Department	can	have	their	own	publication	team	put	the	document	into	the	format	that	is	congruous	with	other	Navajo	County	Public	Health	Department	documents.	The	Final	CHA	will	take	one-two	weeks	to	draft.		*If	CCS	uses	their	own	graphic	designer,	an	additional	$2,000-$4000	will	be	added	to	the	contract.	6)		Draft	CHIP:	(4-6	weeks)		CCS	will	develop	a	CHIP	that	contains	Navajo	County’s	Vision,	Goals,	Outcomes	and	actionable	strategies.	The	CHIP	will	also	include	an	executive	summary,	discussion	about	guiding	principles	and	frameworks,	updated	policies	and	legislation,	community	strengths	and	challenges,	community	resources	and	acknowledgement	of	all	the	contributors.	CCS	will	develop	the	CHA	through	the	compilation	of	input	and	feedback	from	the	planning	meetings,	the	environmental	scan	and	data	review	from	the	CHA,	any	additional	information	collected	during	the	planning	process	or	from	other	Navajo	County	subject	specific	planning	groups.	Once	a	draft	CHIP	is	developed	an	arduous	review	process	will	be	developed	for	the	Navajo	County	Planning	Team	and	other	Navajo	County	Public	Health	partners	to	provide	additional	input	and/or	revisions.		7)	Develop	Final	CHIP:	(2-4	weeks)		CCS	will	develop	the	final	CHIP	after	they	have	collected	feedback	from	the	planning	team	on	the	draft	CHIP.	The	final	CHA	report	will	be	delivered	electronically	in	both	MS	Word	and	PDF	formats.		*If	CCS	uses	their	own	graphic	designer,	an	additional	$2,000-$4000	will	be	added	to	the	contract.

CONTRACT FOR PROFESSIONAL SERVICES
Agreement made as of December 1, 2017 between Navajo County Public Health Services 
District (“County”) and Mare Insights (“Consultant”).
Whereas, the County requires certain professional services to provide leadership in the 
development of the community health assessment (CHA) and Community health improvement 
plan (CHIP).
Whereas, Consultant represents that it has the necessary expertise to provide such services in 
accordance with the terms of this Agreement.
Now, Therefore, the parties agree as follows:
1. Services.  The following services will be conducted to include Public Health Accreditation 
Boards standards where applicable and as outlined in the proposal:
1. Community meeting planning meeting
i. Identify the Team and Resources
a. Finalize work plan
b. Set regular status update meetings
c. Identify assessment advisory group 
2. Focus groups on health issues (one in south county and one in north county)
i. Defining the Purpose and Scope
a. Schedule focus groups, public forums, and community meetings in 
a draft work plan
b. Define end users of the CHA and geographic areas to be included
c. Request datasets that require lead-time
d. Conduct two community meetings to review draft work plan, get 
input, determine when and how to select priorities and adapt plan 
accordingly. 
3. Collecting and Analyzing Data
i. Gather existing epidemiological data, request from identified sources 
additional data sets
ii. Gather existing local public health system data
iii. Request data from Arizona Medical Board

<!-- page 461 -->

iv. Draft focus group discussion guide for review and edit
v. Conduct two focus groups (1 in south and 1 in north county)
vi. Complete report of focus group findings
4. CHA review Community Meetings (one in south county and one in north county)
i. Selecting Priorities
a. Preview CHA and get feedback
b. Set criteria and boundaries for priorities
c. Identify existing programs related to priorities and what gaps exist
5. CHIP Final Report
i. Documenting and Communicating Results
a. Send final draft of Navajo County Community Health Assessment 
incorporating comments as directed by NCPHSD and Partners
b. Complete Navajo County Community Health Assessment, 
incorporating review comments by community, advisory group and 
directed by NCPHSD staff and partners
c. Hold two public forums to announce CHA to public
6. CHIP final report
i. Planning for Action and Monitoring Progress
a. Include achievable goals, objectives, strategies, performance 
measures and time frames
2. Consultant’s Expertise.  Consultant warrants that it has the ability, authority, capacity and 
professional expertise to perform this agreement.  Consultant shall self-perform this 
agreement and be devoted to the successful accomplishment of the work to be performed 
hereunder.  Once work commences on this agreement, consultant shall adhere to agreed 
upon timeline and commitment to successful completion of the contracted services.
3. Independent Contractor.  Consultant acknowledges that it is an independent contractor.  
Neither party is or shall be an agent, employee, partner, joint venturer or associate of the 
other.  An employee or agent of one party shall not be deemed an employer or agent of the 
other party for any purpose.  Taxes or Social Security contributions will not be withheld from 
any payment by the County, and Consultant shall be solely responsible for such matters.
4. Subcontracts.  Consultant shall not enter into any subcontract with respect to any of the 
work to be performed hereunder without the County’s prior written approval.  All 
subcontracts shall comply with applicable federal and state laws and regulations and shall 
impose on the subcontractor substantially the same obligations as are imposed on 
Consultant by this Agreement with respect to those matters covered by Sections 8, 9, 10,

currently practicing under similar circumstances.  Consultant’s services shall be performed 
as expeditiously as is consistent with professional skill and care and the orderly progress of

Services hereunder.  Time limits established by a schedule approved by the County shall 
not, except for reasonable cause, be exceeded by County or Consultant.  The schedule 
shall be adjusted, if necessary, as the Project proceeds.  The date for final performance 
shall be extended by the number of days that performance is delayed by governmental 
approval or review procedures or other causes beyond the Consultant’s reasonable control, 
as jointly confirmed in writing by the parties’ representatives.

8. Defects in Work. County may reject any work product that fails to meet customary 
professional standards or Project specifications.  Consultant agrees to promptly remedy all 
such deficiencies.  The parties shall make a good faith effort to resolve any controversy or 
claim through informal negotiation as set forth in Section 12.  No compensation shall be paid 
for any rejected work until such issues have been resolved.
9. Insurance Requirements.  There are no insurance requirements for this contract.
10. Indemnity.  To the fullest extent allowable by law, Consultant shall defend, indemnify and 
hold harmless County and its representatives, officers, officials and employees (the 
“Indemnitees”) from and against all claims, damages, losses and expenses (including but 
not limited to attorneys fees, court costs, and the costs of appellate proceedings) relating to, 
arising out of or resulting from Consultant’s negligent acts, errors, mistakes or omissions in 
the performance of this Agreement.  Consultant’s duty to defend, hold harmless and 
indemnify the Indemnitees shall arise in connection with any claim, damage, loss or 
expense that is attributable to bodily injury, sickness, disease, death or injury to, impairment 
or destruction of property including loss of use resulting therefrom cause whole or in part by 
any negligent act, error, mistake or omission in the performance of this Agreement (including 
those by any person for whose negligent acts, errors, mistakes or omissions Consultant may 
be liable) to the extent of Consultant’s negligence or fault.  The amount and type of 
insurance coverage requirements set forth above shall not limit the scope of the indemnity in 
this paragraph.
11. Records.  Consultant shall retain, and shall require each subcontractor (if applicable) to 
retain, all books, accounts, reports, files and other records relating to this Agreement for a 
period of five years after completion of the work.  All such documents shall be subject to 
inspection and audit by County upon reasonable notice during normal business hours.  A 
legible copy of any or all such documents shall be produced by Consultant at the request 
and expense of County.
12. Non-A ssignment.Consultant shall not assign any right in this Agreement without County’s 
prior written approval, nor shall Consultant delegate any duty hereunder without County’s 
prior written approval.
13. Negotiation of Disputes. The parties shall make a good faith effort to resolve any claim or 
controversy or claim through informal negotiation.  Notice of any claim or controversy shall 
be provided in writing, with supporting documentation, to the recipient designated in Section 
21.  The recipient shall have seven calendar days to prepare and deliver a written response.

14. Suspension and Termination by County:
1. Suspension.  County may, without cause, order Consultant in writing to suspend, delay 
or interrupt its performance in whole or part.  An adjustment shall be made to the 
completion date and for any increase in cost of performance resulting from the 
suspension, delay or interruption.  No adjustment shall be made to the extent that 
performance is or would have been suspended, delayed or interrupted by another cause 
for which Consultant is responsible.
2. Termination for Cause.  County may terminate this Agreement for cause if Consultant 
refuses or fails to perform this Agreement; disregards laws, ordinances, rules, 
regulations or orders of a public authority having jurisdiction; or otherwise commits a 
material breach of this Agreement.  When any of the foregoing causes exist, County 
shall give Consultant written notice and a ten-day opportunity to cure the default.  If the 
default remains uncured, County may (without prejudice to any rights or remedies it may 
have) terminate this Agreement and finish the work or cause it to be finished.  If the 
unpaid balance of the Agreement exceeds the cost of finishing the work (including 
expenses incurred by the County as a result of the Consultant’s default), Consultant 
shall be entitled to payment for its performance up to the amount of such excess.  If the 
cost of finishing the work exceeds the unpaid balance, Consultant shall pay the 
difference to County.
3. Termination for Convenience. County, by written notice to Consultant, may terminate 
this Agreement in whole or part when in the sole discretion of County it is in County’s 
best interests to do so.  Consultant shall be paid for all material, equipment and services 
provided, as well as reasonable termination expenses and a reasonable allowance from 
profit and overhead, provided that such payments, exclusive of termination expenses, 
shall not exceed the total amount payable pursuant to Section 6 less any payments 
previously made to Consultant.  Consultant shall not be entitled to profit and overhead 
on material, equipment and services which were not provided.
4. Consultant’s Responsibility.  Upon receipt of a termination notice, Consultant shall (a) 
promptly discontinue all services (unless notice directs otherwise), and (b) deliver or 
otherwise make available to County copies of all data, reports, summaries and other

15. Termination by Consultant.  Consultant may terminate this Agreement upon written notice 
to County if performance is made impossible for a period of thirty (30) consecutive calendar 
days for any of the following reasons through no act or fault of Consultant or its agents, 
employees or subcontractors:  issuance of an order by a count or other public authority 
having jurisdiction; an act of government, such as a declaration of national emergency; or a 
natural disaster or other Act of God.  Consultant may also terminate this Agreement upon 
written notice and a ten-day opportunity to cure if County fails to make any payment within 
time set forth in Section 6.  Consultant may also terminate this Agreement upon written 
notice if suspensions, delays or interruptions by County equal in the aggregate more than 
100% of the total number of days scheduled for completion.
16. Governing Law.  This Agreement shall be governed by the law of the State of Arizona.  Any 
suit arising out of this Agreement shall be brought in the state courts of Arizona (with venue 
in Navajo County) or federal District of Arizona, but only after informal negotiation pursuant 
to Section 12.
17. Incorporation of Applicable Laws. Every provision of law required by statute or regulation 
to be in this Agreement will be read and enforced as though included herein.  Each party 
shall promptly notify the other upon discovery that any such provision has been omitted.
18. Non-Collusion.  The provisions A.R.S. 38-511 are incorporated herein by this reference.
19. One-Year Limitation on Actions.  No action shall be maintained by Consultant on any 
claim based upon or arising out of this Agreement unless such action is commenced within 
one year after the County’s final payment hereunder.
20. Entire Agreement. This Agreement and those documents incorporated by reference 
represent the entire understanding between the parties.  No amendment shall be effective 
unless executed by both parties with the same formality as this Agreement.  Provided, 
however, that the County’s designated representative is authorized to modify the Scope of 
Work, in writing, with the concurrence of Consultant so long as the project is not changed 
substantially or significant additional compensation is not required.

Board of Supervisors Regular 6.a.        
Meeting Date: 12/12/2017  
Title: Purchase of a 2017 New Caterpillar CB66B Asphalt Compactor, Double Drum
Submitted By: Melissa Buckley, Clerk of the Board of Supervisors
Department: Board of Supervisors
Presented By: Jeanine Carruthers 
Motion before the Board:
Consideration of the purchase of a 2017 New Caterpillar CB66B Asphalt Compactor,Double Drum
from Empire Machinery in the amount of $187,886.56.
Background:
The City of Tucson has a contract with Caterpillar, Inc. for the purchase and/or lease of heavy
equipment.  Empire Machinery is a dealer for Caterpillar, Inc. and this contract is available for the
use of public and nonprofit agencies that have entered into a Cooperative Purchasing Agreement
with the City of Tucson's Department of Procurement or registered with the National IPA. 
According to the City of Tucson's website, Navajo County is a Cooperative Partner and we are
also registered with the National IPA.  Public Works budgeted $250,000 for this item and the cost
came in at $187,886.56 which is approximately a savings of $62,000.  Staff feels that the price
under this contract is fair and being that Empire is a local dealer in Show Low we have easy
access for any warranty repairs and/or needed parts. 
Recommendation: 
Staff recommends approval 
Fiscal Impact
Fiscal Year: FY2018
Budgeted Y/N: Y
Amount Requested: 187,886.56
Fiscal Impact:
The Public Works department budgeted $250,000 for this item and the quote came in under budget
with a cost savings of approximately $62,000.00.
